=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a grid with 10^9 rows and W columns. The cell at the x-th column from the left and the y-th row from the bottom is denoted by (x,y).
There are N blocks. Each block is a 1 \times 1 square, and block i-th (1 \leq i \leq N) is located at cell (X_i,Y_i) at time 0.
At times t=1,2,\dots,10^{100}, the blocks are moved according to the following rules:

- If the entire bottom row is filled with blocks, then all blocks in the bottom row are removed.
- For each remaining block, in order from bottom to top, perform the following:
- If the block is in the bottom row, or if there is a block in the cell immediately below it, do nothing.
- Otherwise, move the block one cell downward.



You are given Q queries. For the j-th query (1 \leq j \leq Q), answer whether block A_j exists at time T_j+0.5.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N W
X_1 Y_1
X_2 Y_2
\vdots
X_N Y_N
Q
T_1 A_1
T_2 A_2
\vdots
T_Q A_Q

Output

Print Q lines. The i-th line should contain Yes if block A_i exists at time T_i+0.5, and No otherwise.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq W \leq N
- 1 \leq X_i \leq W
- 1 \leq Y_i \leq 10^9
- (X_i,Y_i) \neq (X_j,Y_j) if i \neq j.
- 1 \leq Q \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq T_j \leq 10^9
- 1 \leq A_j \leq N
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 3
1 1
1 2
2 2
3 2
2 3
6
1 1
1 2
2 3
2 5
3 4
3 5

Sample Output 1

Yes
Yes
No
Yes
No
Yes

The positions of the blocks change as follows: ("時刻" means "time.")


- Query 1: At time 1.5, block 1 exists, so the answer is Yes.
- Query 2: At time 1.5, block 2 exists, so the answer is Yes.
- Query 3: Block 3 disappears at time 2, so it does not exist at time 2.5, and the answer is No.

Sample Input 2

3 2
1 1
2 1
1 2
4
1 1
1 2
1 3
2 3

Sample Output 2

No
No
Yes
Yes

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's analyze column dynamics more deeply.

At any time, within a column, blocks fall downward one cell per step if the cell immediately below is empty at the time of processing. This is equivalent to each block falls with speed 1 until it is directly above another block (or bottom row), then stops; if lower block later removed, it resumes falling. This is like particles in 1D with exclusion, falling under gravity. The bottommost block falls until y=1, then waits. The second block falls until it is just above the bottom block (i.e., y=2), then waits, etc. Thus at any time, after enough falling, blocks in a column form a contiguous stack from y=1 up to some height? Wait if bottom block at y=1 and second block has fallen to y=2, third to y=3, etc. But if second block is far above, it may still be falling; the set is not contiguous but the lower part is contiguous up to some height, with upper blocks in transit. More generally, due to exclusion and gravity, the blocks in a column can be described by their "heights" decreasing over time; they cannot pass; the gap between consecutive blocks decreases? So recurrence seems plausible! Need prove.

Let's formalize.

Let y_{c,r} be initial Y coordinate of r-th lowest block in column c (if exists). Let E_r be time of r-th bottom-row removal, if it occurs. If some column has fewer than r blocks, then no r-th event. For r=1..M where M = min_c m_c, events occur. We claim:
E_1 = max_c y_{c,1}.
For r >= 1, if E_r exists and all columns have at least r+1 blocks, then
E_{r+1} = max( E_r + 1, max_c y_{c,r+1} ).
Equivalently E_r = max_{k<=r} ( max_c y_{c,k} + (r-k) )? Let's solve recurrence:
E_1 = M1 = max_c y_{c,1}.
E_{r+1} = max(E_r + 1, M_{r+1}) where M_k = max_c y_{c,k}.
Unroll:
E_r = max_{k=1..r} ( M_k + (r-k) ), with E_1 = M1 = max(M1+0). Also maybe E_r >= r? Since y>=1, M_k>=1, so M_k+r-k >= r? If M_k>=1, yes. This closed form may be useful. Let's verify W=1 y=[5,10,100]: M1=5,M2=10,M3=100. E2=max(5+1=6,10+0=10)=10; formula max(5+1,10)=10. E3=max(5+2=7,10+1=11,100)=100. Good.

Now, if this recurrence is correct, the solution becomes easy:
- Group blocks by column, sort by Y ascending.
- Compute M = min number of blocks in any column? But if any column has 0 blocks initially, no events (M=0). Since W<=N but columns may be empty. If any empty, no bottom row full ever, so all blocks survive forever. Answer all Yes. Wait if a column empty initially, no block can ever appear in it, so bottom row never full. Yes no removals. So all queries Yes. Is that consistent with recurrence? M=0, no events. Good.
- If all columns nonempty, M = min column size.
- For each rank r=1..M, compute M_r = max over columns of the Y coordinate of the r-th lowest block in that column. Need all columns have at least r blocks for r<=M. Then E_r = max_{k=1..r} (M_k + r - k). Or iterative E_r = max(E_{r-1}+1, M_r). Since M up to 2e5, can compute array E[1..M].
- For each block, its rank r in its column. If r <= M and E_r <= T? Then removed at time E_r. If r > M or no events, survives. Actually if r<=M, block is removed at the r-th bottom-removal event, time E_r. If T >= E_r, at time T+0.5 gone. If T < E_r, exists. If r>M, no event removes it, exists forever (after all events stop; but could it be removed later? No because a column empty after M events, so bottom row never full; no more removals). So answer No if r<=M and E_r <= T, else Yes.

So event times are just M_r = max_c y_{c,r}. This is a very elegant result. Let's prove directly maybe.

Why E_r = max_c y_{c,r}? It means the r-th block from bottom in each column is removed at time equal to the maximum initial Y among all r-th blocks. This is like each block's "natural" fall time if unobstructed? Let's understand.

For a block with initial height y, if it were alone, it would reach bottom at time y and be removed at time y (if bottom row full? For W=1). With multiple blocks, the r-th block's removal time is max over columns of initial y_{c,r}. It does not depend on lower blocks except through rank. Is that always true? The lower block removal doesn't delay it; it just allows it to move into bottom at the "free fall" time. In general, the r-th block falls at speed 1 whenever below empty; it may be blocked by lower block if it catches up. But if it catches up, it waits; after lower block removed, it resumes. Does it still reach y=1 at time y_{c,r}? Let's test a case where rank2 initially close behind rank1: c1 y1=100, y2=101. Rank1 removed at time100? M1 maybe 100. Rank2 initial y101. Thus proof: The r-th event time is max_c y_{c,r}. We can prove by induction using recurrence E_r = M_r. Need prove recurrence rigorously.

Let's attempt induction.

Define state before r-th removal. We want show E_r = M_r = max_c y_{c,r}. Base r=1: The lowest block in column c initially at y_{c,1}. It falls one cell per step when below empty; it reaches y=1 after y_{c,1}-1 steps. Thus all columns have bottom row full at time M_1 -1? So event time M_1. Good.

Inductive step: Assume E_{r-1}=M_{r-1}. Need show E_r = M_r. At time E_{r-1}, the (r-1)-th blocks are removed. Consider the r-th block in column c. Now, let's think about memory/time. N,Q up to 2e5. Python code:
import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N,W = map(int,input().split())
    cols=[[] for _ in range(W+1)]
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        x,y=map(int,input().split())
        cols[x].append((y,i))
    # sort each col
    for c in range(1,W+1):
        cols[c].sort(key=lambda p:p[0])
    # assign rank and compute M
    M = min(len(cols[c]) for c in range(1,W+1))
    rank = [0]*(N+1)
    for c in range(1,W+1):
        for r,(y,idx) in enumerate(cols[c], start=1):
            rank[idx]=r
    # If M==0, all queries Yes. But still need read Q and output.
    Q=int(input())
    out=[]
    if M==0:
        for _ in range(Q):
            input() # T A
            out.append("Yes")
    else:
        # compute event_time for ranks 1..M
        event_time=[0]*(M+1)
        # M_r = max over columns of cols[c][r-1][0]
        # Since W*M <= N? If M>0, W<=N, W*M <= N? Actually each column has at least M blocks, so W*M <= N. Yes.
        # We can compute by iterating r:
        for r in range(1,M+1):
            mx=0
            for c in range(1,W+1):
                y=cols[c][r-1][0]
                if y>mx: mx=y
            event_time[r]=mx
        # read queries
        for _ in range(Q):
            T,A=map(int,input().split())
            r=rank[A]
            if r<=M and T >= event_time[r]:
                out.append("No")
            else:
                out.append("Yes")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
if __name__=="__main__": main()

Let's write code:

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N_W = input().split()
    if not N_W: return
    N = int(N_W[0]); W = int(N_W[1])
    cols = [[] for _ in range(W+1)]
    for i in range(1, N+1):
        x,y = map(int, input().split())
        cols[x].append((y,i))
    M = min(len(cols[c]) for c in range(1, W+1))
    Q = int(input())
    if M == 0:
        out = ["Yes"] * Q
        for _ in range(Q):
            input()
        sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
        return

    for c in range(1, W+1):
        cols[c].sort()
    rank = [0]*(N+1)
    for c in range(1, W+1):
        for r, (y, idx) in enumerate(cols[c], 1):
            rank[idx] = r

    event_time = [0]*(M+1)
    for r in range(1, M+1):
        mx = 0
        for c in range(1, W+1):
            y = cols[c][r-1][0]
            if y > mx:
                mx = y
        event_time[r] = mx

    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        T,A = map(int, input().split())
        r = rank[A]
        if r <= M and T >= event_time[r]:
            out.append("No")
        else:
            out.append("Yes")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's write final code with comments maybe:

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, W = map(int, input().split())
    cols = [[] for _ in range(W + 1)]
    for idx in range(1, N + 1):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        cols[x].append((y, idx))

    min_blocks = min(len(cols[c]) for c in range(1, W + 1))
    Q = int(input())

    if min_blocks == 0:
        sys.stdout.write("\n".join(["Yes"] * Q))
        return

    for c in range(1, W + 1):
        cols[c].sort()

    rank = [0] * (N + 1)
    for c in range(1, W + 1):
        for r, (_, idx) in enumerate(cols[c], 1):
            rank[idx] = r

    # deletion_time[r] = time of r-th deletion of bottom row
    deletion_time = [0] * (min_blocks + 1)
    for c in range(1, W + 1):
        col = cols[c]
        for r in range(1, min_blocks + 1):
            y = col[r - 1][0]
            if y > deletion_time[r]:
                deletion_time[r] = y

    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        T, a = map(int, input().split())
        r = rank[a]
        if r <= min_blocks and T >= deletion_time[r]:
            out.append("No")
        else:
            out.append("Yes")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "order from bottom to top" if a block at y=3 and below y=2 occupied by a block that moved down to y=1, then below y=2 empty, so block at y=3 moves down. We used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "blocks are moved" maybe all blocks move simultaneously? But statement explicitly says in order from bottom to top, so not simultaneous. We used.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden problem with "If the entire bottom row is filled with blocks, then all blocks in the bottom row are removed." If bottom row full, after removal, some columns may have no blocks. Then in the same step, no blocks in those columns to move. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "min_blocks" if a column has zero blocks and M=0, but bottom row could be filled if the empty column's bottom cell is considered filled by "empty"? No.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "Y_i" up to 1e9 and "T_j" up to 1e9, but event_time maybe 1e9 and T=1e9 -> No. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "deletion_time" if using direct max and M_r maybe not sorted due to same Y? We proved strict. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden problem with "N=2e5, W=2e5, min_blocks=1" and event_time loop over columns for r=1 only; good. If min_blocks=1 and W=2e5, W*M=2e5. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "sort" if a column has many blocks; sorting by tuple sorts by y then idx. If same y impossible, but if same y in same column impossible. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "rank" if a block's Y is lower but index higher; rank by Y. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "block A_j exists at time T_j+0.5" if it was removed at time E but T_j = E and there is a possibility it is removed after movement at time E, not before? The rule removes before movement, so at time E+0.5 gone. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden problem with "At times t=1,2,\dots,10^{100}" if a block removed at time E, at time E+0.5 gone. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "time T_j+0.5" if T_j=1 and event_time=1, at time1.5 after step1, removed. Good.

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline

    N, W = map(int, input().split())
    cols = [[] for _ in range(W + 1)]

    for idx in range(1, N + 1):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        cols[x].append((y, idx))

    min_blocks = min(len(cols[c]) for c in range(1, W + 1))
    Q = int(input())

    # If some column is empty, the bottom row can never become full.
    if min_blocks == 0:
        sys.stdout.write("\n".join(["Yes"] * Q))
        return

    for c in range(1, W + 1):
        cols[c].sort()

    # rank[i] = position of block i from the bottom in its column
    rank = [0] * (N + 1)
    for c in range(1, W + 1):
        for r, (_, idx) in enumerate(cols[c], 1):
            rank[idx] = r

    # deletion_time[r] = time when the r-th bottom-row deletion happens
    deletion_time = [0] * (min_blocks + 1)
    for c in range(1, W + 1):
        col = cols[c]
        for r in range(1, min_blocks + 1):
            y = col[r - 1][0]
            if y > deletion_time[r]:
                deletion_time[r] = y

    ans = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        T, a = map(int, input().split())
        r = rank[a]
        if r <= min_blocks and T >= deletion_time[r]:
            ans.append("No")
        else:
            ans.append("Yes")

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
